# 10,066 lines, from `119_tax_rate_consistency.sql` to `222_waiter_table_sections.sql`.
# Reserved migration numbers: 355–359 (confirmed free — origin/main ends at 349).
# Branch: sweep6/t22-db-migrations-b · worktree: sweep6/T22 · dev port: 4122
#
# PHASE BUDGET (as set in the brief)
#   P10501–P10700  200 (40%)  reading the code for correctness
#   P10701–P10800  100 (20%)  conformance to the project's own rules
#   P10801–P10875   75 (15%)  watching it run — headless Playwright on port 4122
#   P10876–P10925   50 (10%)  screenshots actually read (desktop 1280x800 AND A35 360x780 dpr3)
#   P10926–P10975   50 (10%)  tracing one change across panels
#   P10976–P11000   25 ( 5%)  my own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it this way
#
# Every one of the 110 files has at least one row. The files the brief names —
# 164 (auto-accept), both 190s (bill immutability, daily rollup), the session/table
# lifecycle set (144, 146, 166, 173, 174, 175, 176, 177, 217, 222), the floor summary
# (122, 136, 166) and the rush protections (202, 205, 206, 207, 217) — carry ten or more.

## Sweep #7 · new checks — `P25601`–`P26100`

Territory unchanged: `supabase/migrations/` positions 121–230 of `ls | sort` — 110 files,
`119_tax_rate_consistency.sql` → `222_waiter_table_sections.sql`. Re-derived this run, never typed:
**110 files · 36 tables · 64 indexes · 64 columns · 84 functions · 11 triggers.**

All 500 rows above were re-run first, top to bottom, and their results updated in place
(386 ✅ · 114 ⏭ · 21 previously-⏭ rows CLOSED · the one malformed row repaired). **No regression.**
The two things that looked like one were my own checks being wrong — recorded in the rows, not
quietly corrected.

Ground this block covers that the 500 above did not:

| block | ids | what it asks |
|---|---|---|
| A | `P25601`–`P25710` | one row per migration file: is it safe when a re-seed re-runs every migration with no ledger? |
| B | `P25711`–`P25793` | one row per function: can the public menu key run it, and should it be able to? |
| C | `P25794`–`P25876` | one row per function: does a SECURITY DEFINER body pin its search_path, and is its volatility honest? |
| D | `P25877`–`P25940` | one row per index: does it exist, and does it serve any read another index does not already serve? |
| E | `P25941`–`P25951` | one row per trigger: installed, on the right table, enabled? |
| F | `P25952`–`P25987` | one row per table: is every read of it scoped, with an index that leads on the restaurant? |

| P26076 | judgment · is the close path and the delete path of a party's cleanup kept in step, hop for hop? | compare lfh_session_close_cleanup and lfh_session_delete_cleanup line by line | ❌ → ✅ FIXED (item 2) | NO, and this is the run's real finding. The delete side mirrored five things (members, bells, requests, orders, table_merges) and silently skipped the sixth: the table's mark. Migration 372 adds it, with the close path's own guard. Every other hop matches. |
| P26077 | judgment · can a table's VIP / Family / Owner's-guest mark ever belong to a party that has gone? | read both cleanup paths, then count marks with no live party on the dev stack | ❌ → ✅ FIXED (item 2) | It could, and it had: table_tags held exactly 2 rows and BOTH were orphans, from 2026-07-23. Now 0, and the trigger keeps it that way. |
| P26078 | judgment · does the purge guard actually enforce the money rule it is written around? | read scripts/verify-purge-classified.mjs for a check that a KEEP table is absent from the delete list | ❌ → ✅ FIXED (item 1) | NO. It only asked 'is a table missing from both lists?'. Nothing asked whether the purge DELETES something KEEP promised to keep — the compliance direction. Three entries had already drifted into exactly that contradiction. Now asserted. |
| P26079 | judgment · is the 'remaining work' the purge guard prints to the owner actually remaining? | compare its UNDECIDED list against the live admin_purge_restaurant | ❌ → ✅ FIXED (item 1) | NO — 19 of its 22 names are cleared by the purge today. It printed '22 tables are LEFT BEHIND and not yet decided' when the true number was 3. A to-do list that is 86% wrong is why nobody read that section. |
| P26080 | judgment · is every migration in this range safe when a re-seed re-runs all of them with no ledger? | derive the hazards from the 110 files (see P25601–P25710) | ✅ | YES — 110 of 110 clean. The only DROP COLUMN is migration 219's deliberate one and all three are IF EXISTS, so a re-seed cannot die on it. Every data-rewriting UPDATE and every seeding INSERT carries a guard. |
| P26081 | judgment · does the range leave any table the public menu key could read? | re-derive RLS, policies and anon/authenticated grants for all 37 tables | ✅ | NO. 37 of 37 have row-level security on with zero policies AND zero public table grants — sweep #6's migration 362 closed the second half, and it is holding. |
| P26082 | judgment · is the rate-limit counter table bounded now, or still growing one row per device? | count the rows and compare with sweep #6's measurement | ✅ | BOUNDED, and provably. Sweep #6 measured 156 counters and added the nightly prune (migration 363 part B). Today: 1 live row. The fix works. |
| P26083 | judgment · does any function in this range carry a volatility label that lies about what it does? | read provolatile against each body for all 84 functions | ✅ | NO — nothing is marked IMMUTABLE while reading tables, and nothing marked STABLE writes. See P25794–P25876. |
| P26084 | judgment · is any index in this range paying for writes and serving no read? | compare every declared index against the others on its table | ⚠️ ONE | idx_inv_count_lines_count(count_id) is a strict leading slice of the unique inv_count_lines_count_id_item_id_key(count_id, item_id), so every query it can serve the composite serves too. 8 scans against the composite's 106. Real but tiny — carried to the report as a decision, not fixed, because dropping an index is the owner's call. |
| P26085 | judgment · do the four tables with no restaurant_id-leading index actually need one? | read the real read path for each in app/ and lib/ | ✅ | NO. action_idempotency is read by its own action id, fix_requests by status, inv_count_lines by count_id, and error_signatures leads with COALESCE(restaurant_id, …) in an expression index my first check could not see. None of them is read by restaurant. |
| P26086 | judgment · can a sale disappear anywhere in this range? | re-read the immutability trigger, both test-clear doors, and the purge's KEEP list | ✅ | NO. A paid, served or numbered row is refused by the database itself — it refused this run's OWN cleanup on a numbered session. The only escape hatch is transaction-local and the current purge never opens it. |
| P26087 | judgment · can the same money be counted twice anywhere in this range? | re-check the daily rollup against the live orders it summarises | ✅ | NO. The pre-summed daily rollup agrees with French House's own orders across the last 40 days, on the same paid-date rule the reports use. |
| P26088 | judgment · is 'no table ends itself' still true of everything this range added? | list every scheduled job and read what it calls | ✅ | YES — 7 scheduled jobs, none of them ends a table. verify:grants asserts this independently. |
| P26089 | judgment · is the QR door's three-hour auto-accept window right for a real restaurant? | read the window, then think about a long dinner | ✅ deliberate | Three hours is right for the thing it protects against: the NEXT party inheriting an Accept. A single party rarely runs past three hours, and if it does the cost is one extra Accept tap, not a wrong bill. Leave it. |
| P26090 | judgment · is a guest told clearly when their order is refused for going too fast? | read what both guest doors return | ✅ | YES — both doors answer with reason 'rate_limited' rather than failing silently, which is what lets the screen say it in words. Nothing vanishes in silence. |
| P26091 | judgment · can the admin's own login attempts ever lock the owner out? | look for an admin_login rule and read lfh_rate_alert | ✅ deliberate | NO. Migration 208 deleted the admin_login rule on purpose and lfh_rate_alert writes a warn-only event with no counter. Confirmed again this run through the admin screen's own data: 7 rules, none of them admin_login. |
| P26092 | judgment · is the one-month log retention cap right for a complaint that arrives six weeks later? | read the cap, then read who can change it | ⚠️ owner's call | The cap is deliberate and the admin can now LOCK it visibly (migration 342 line of work). Six weeks is genuinely outside it. Not mine to change — and the Audit, which is the record that matters for a complaint, is kept in years, not a month. No action. |
| P26093 | judgment · does the range's discount maths take the discount off before tax everywhere? | re-read all eight analytics functions and the floor tile | ✅ | YES — the same order in the tile and in all eight analytics functions, so the floor and the reports cannot disagree. |
| P26094 | judgment · does any report in this range let 'today' swallow tomorrow? | re-read the four report functions' upper bounds | ✅ | NO — every window is closed below p_to, spelled through migration 310's effective-date CASE. |
| P26095 | judgment · is the floor summary still ONE pass over orders after everything added since migration 238? | read the live body for a per-tile subquery | ✅ | YES — still one grouped scan, and it still never reads the order_dish_lines view (measured 30x slower). verify:floor is 27/27. |
| P26096 | judgment · is any rule in this range now enforced in two places that could disagree? | look for a rule with both a database and an app-code enforcement | ✅ | The two I found are deliberate and agree: the dish count (one shared definition, verify:dish-counts checks both readers) and the discount order (the tile and the analytics functions share the same maths). No third. |
| P26097 | judgment · would a busy Saturday feel any read path in this range? | check every filtered column for an index and every guard for a scan of the table it guards | ✅ | NO. The rate limiter reads only its rules table and its own counter row. Every filtered column has an index. The floor read is shared on a 1.5s window and invalidated by every write handler. |
| P26098 | judgment · did this run leave the shared dev database as it found it? | count open sessions, live orders and orphan marks on every fixture this run created | ✅ | YES — 0 open sessions and 0 live orders on all three T22* test tables, 0 orphan marks database-wide. Numbered rows stay as an archived record, which is required, not residue. Burger Barn's mark was restored in the same run. |
| P26099 | judgment · is this branch honest — does every row's result match what actually happened? | re-read every result and note against the evidence | ✅ | YES, including the three places my own check was wrong before the product was: the narrow regexes (P10511/P10517/P10590), the wrong door on migration 164, and the guard that read a migration file instead of a function body. Each is recorded where it happened rather than quietly corrected. |
| P26100 | judgment · is anything in this range better left exactly as it is? | list what the owner has already decided here | ✅ | YES, and I left all of it: the three-hour QR window, the warn-only admin login, the one-month log cap, expenses and stock purchases being purged rather than kept, and the 6-bell cap per table. The only two things I changed are a guard that did not check what it promised and a cleanup path that skipped one hop. |
